.
∎

Lemma C.11 (Preservation of Well-formedness).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
be a global type.
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
is well-formed with respect to some router
s
,
i.e.
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
.

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}.~\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}\Longrightarrow\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)\right)

Proof.

By rule induction on
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}
.

For each transition, we show the two conjuncts for
well-formedness,
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
:

(1)

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists for
r

such that
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists;
and,
(2)

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

•

[
Gr1
],
where

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\to\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {

}\!\!\rightsquigarrow\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {

}\!\!\rightsquigarrow\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}=G_{j}}}
,

{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}?{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}
.

(1)

We know
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
by assumption.
To show
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
, consider
r
by case:

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}}
, which exists.

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\with\left\{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{i}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}
,
so
\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}.~{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{i}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}
exists.

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}
, which exists as
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j\in I}}
.

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}\notin\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}\}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
,
which exists.

(2)

We know
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
by assumption.
We deduce
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
by consequence.

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\Longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\in\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}\}\wedge{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\Longrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}

[
Gr3
],
where
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}
.
By hypothesis,
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}
.

We first show that
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)

(1)

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists for some
r
.

Note that
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists regardless of
r
’s
participation in
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
.

–

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}\in{\textnormal{{pt}}\left({{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\right)}
,
then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}}}
,
so
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists.

–

Otherwise,
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\texttt{end}}}
, which exists.

Projection is homomorphic under recursion, so

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
exists.

(2)

By assumption,
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}})}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
,
so
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

The
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{}}}}
relation is also homomorphic under recursion,
so we get
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

We conclude by induction to obtain
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left(G^{\prime},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
.

,
we get

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}.~({{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{i}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}\text{ exists }\wedge{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{i}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}})
.

(1)

To show
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
, consider
r
by case:

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{j}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}}
.

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\with\!\langle{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!}\rangle\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{i}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}
.

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\looparrowright\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{i}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}
.

–

{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}\notin\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\}
:
Then
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}={{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{j}}}}\!\mathrel{\upharpoonright}\!{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}}
.

(2)

We have
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{t}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}

from assumption and

{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}_{j}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
from induction, so
{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}\circledast{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

∎

Lemma C.12 (Progress for Well-formed Global Types).

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
be a global type.
Suppose
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
is well-formed with respect to some router
s
,
i.e.
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
.

({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\texttt{end}}})\vee\exists{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}.~(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}})

Proof.

The following is logically equivalent:

({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G\neq{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\texttt{end}}}}})\Longrightarrow\exists{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}.~(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}).

We prove this by induction on the structure of
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
.

We do not consider
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\texttt{end}}}}}
by assumption.

We do not consider
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}}}
as the type variable occurs
free.

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G^{\prime\prime}}}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}
must occur in
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
,
so
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}}\neq{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\texttt{end}}}
.

By induction,

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}.~(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G[{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}/{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mathbf{t}}}]}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}})
.

Apply [
Gr3
] to get

\exists{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}},{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}.~(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\mu\mathbf{t}.G}}}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}})
.

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\to\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}

Apply [
Gr1
] to get

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[3.5em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\rightsquigarrow\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}}}
.

(3)

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!-\mspace{-3mu}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{r}}}}
}\!\!}\!\shortrightarrow\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}

By assumption,
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

Apply [
Gr6
] to get

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[3.5em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\texttt{via}{\langle\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!\rangle}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}!{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}})}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\underset{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!}{\rightsquigarrow}\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}}}
.

(4)

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\rightsquigarrow\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}

Apply [
Gr2
] to get

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[3.5em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}?{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}}
.

(5)

{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G={\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{p}}}}
}\!\!\underset{\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{r}}}}
}\!\!}{\rightsquigarrow}\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{q}}}}
}\!\!.~{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}:\left\{{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l_{i}}}\!:\!G_{i}}\right\}_{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{i\in I}}}}}}}

By assumption,
\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)
, so
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{r}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}
.

Apply [
Gr7
] to get

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[3.5em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{{\texttt{via}{\langle\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!\rangle}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{p}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{q}}}}?{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{j}}}})}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G_{j}}}}}
.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.11

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}
be a global type, and
s
be a role. Then we have:

\textnormal{{wellFormed}}\left({\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\textnormal{{wellFormed}}^{R}\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrm{\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}},~\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!\rrbracket},{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}{\textbf{{s}}}}\right)

Proof.

(
\Longrightarrow
) Direct consequence of

Lemmas

C.9
and
C.3

(
\Longleftarrow
) By definition.
∎

C.4.
Proof of
Theorem

4.12

Let
{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G,G^{\prime}}}
be well-formed global types
such that
\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}
for some label
{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}

\forall{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}},\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!.~\left(\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}}\xrightarrow{\mathmakebox[1em]{{\color[rgb]{0.5,0,0.5}{l}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}}}\Longleftrightarrow\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrm{\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G}}},~\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {

}\!\!\rrbracket}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathrm{\llbracket}{{\color[rgb]{0.816,0.125,0.565}{G^{\prime}}}},~\!\!\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5} {
{{s}}}}
}\!\!\rrbracket}}}}}\right)

s

Appendix D
Appendix for
Section

5

We include
two more studies:
Battleships

and
Travel Agency
.

Battleships

We implement the
Battleships
board gamebetween two players, as used by
King
et al. 2019
.
The initialisation phase involves both players
placing rectangular battleships on a 2D grid.
The session proceeds to the game loop,
where players take turns to guess the location of their
opponent’s ships, where the server responds with
a
hit
or a
miss
.
The game loop continues until all ships of one player
have been sunk.

⬇

By interpreting the session ID (generated for
Svr

by
STScript
) as an unique game identifier,
the developer can keep track of
concurrent game sessions very easily.
As the generated session runtime for
Node.js
needs to be
initialised by a
function
that is parameterised by
the game ID and returns the initial state,
the game ID is bound inside the closure. This means that
the invocation of callbacks (as part of the game logic)
can access the game ID of the current game, so the
developer can update the application state of the
corresponding game.

Travel Agency

We implement the Travel Agency scenario
motivated in
Section

1
.
We specify the Scribble protocol
in
Fig.

4
, and present
the
STScript
APIs implemented by
the developer for both server and client endpoints
in
Section

2
.

This scenario involves routed communications
– namely, between the customer and their friend.
In
Section

3
, we discuss how the
routing mechanism is transparent to the
Node.js
callbacks
implemented by the developer.
Here, we show that the routing mechanism is equally
transparent to the
React.js
components implemented
by the developer for browser-side endpoints.

Focusing on the role
A
,
the
WaitSuggestion
component expects a message
from the other client role
B
, and
the
WaitResponse
component expects to receive
from the server role
S
.
Because the client roles
A
and
B

cannot directly communicate over a
WebSocket connection, the message to be received by

WaitSuggestion
is in fact routed by
S
.
However, this is transparent to the developer
– both components
handle incoming messages in the same manner.

Additionally, the APIs generated by
STScript
offer developers the flexibility to integrate
existing third-party libraries and frameworks
when designing their user interfaces.
The client endpoints are written using
the
Material-UI
framework
(
Material-UI 2020
)

and leverage the
React Context API

to manage internal application state.

namespace Message

EFSM state transitions are characterised by the
possible
messages
to be sent or received.
We generate an
interface
for each message,
specifying types for the label (as a string literal)
and payload (as defined on the protocol).
Hence, each state is defined
as a
union type
of the possible messages.

namespace Handler

Handlers define the callback-style APIs that
the developer needs to implement.
The handler for a
send state
(i.e.
S40
)
is a tuple of the message label (as a string literal),
payload, and the successor state.
The handler for a
receive state
(i.e.

S38
,
S41
) is an object literal
defining labelled callbacks.
Each callback is parameterised by
a factory function for the successor state
(discussed shortly) and the payload for this
particular message type, and is expected to return
the successor state.
The
MaybePromise<>
generic type allows
developers to write
asynchronous
handlers
in their implementation.

⬇

namespace State

The handler for each EFSM state is used to
instantiate its
State
class instance,
which is used by the session runtime to trigger
the communication action.
For
send states
, the runtime triggers the
performSend

method (
Section

E.1
)
to perform the send using the label and payload
defined in the handler (
Section

E.1
).
For
receive states
, the
State
class instance

registers
the handler to the runtime, so the handler
can be invoked when the message is received.
The
State
class will check whether the handler
is defined asynchronously (i.e. a
TypeScript

Promise
)
and invoke the handler accordingly.

namespace Factory

The
Factory
namespace exposes
developer-friendly

APIs for instantiating the
State
class instance.
The factory API for a
send state

is an object literal defining labelled callbacks for each
possible selection. Each callback is parameterised by the
message payload and successor state.
The factory API for a
receive state
is an alias
for the constructor function of the corresponding
State
class.

Initial
and
Terminal
aliases are also exported as
convenient references to the initial and terminal EFSM state
respectively.

E.2.
Server Role
S
: API Usage

The developer instantiates the session (
Section

E.2
)
using the WebSocket server, cancellation handler,
and the EFSM implementation — a function to be invoked
for every new session, parameterised by the session ID,
and returns the

Section

implements the API generated for
a receive state, specifying how to handle
a

message.

implements the API generated for
a send state — send the
Available

message with the price and proceed to the

handleResponse

src/TravelAgency/B/S29.tsx

The generated React component for a
receive state

registers a message handler (
Section

E.3
)
to the session runtime component,
to be invoked on a WebSocket
onmessage
event.
An abstract method is exposed for each possible branch
(
Sections

E.3
and
E.3
),
requiring the developer to explicitly handle the received
message.
When invoked, the message handler parses
the WebSocket message and invokes the abstract method
(implemented by the developer) corresponding to the
label of the received message.
The message handler returns the successor state to the
runtime to advance the EFSM.
